下次發布日期：115 年 11 月 11 日下午 4 時

115年 9月全國賦稅收入初步統計 
一、全國賦稅收入 
（一）本月數 
9 月實徵淨額 4,965 億元，較上年同月增加 813 億元(+19.6%)。 
（二）累計數 
累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額 3 兆 5,714 億元，較上年同期增加 7,004 億
元(+24.4%)。 
（三）達成率 
1.中央政府總預算業於 9 月 18 日公布，稅課收入較原預算案提高
6,000 億元，併計地方政府及特種基金後，全國稅課收入較預算案
提高 5,852 億元，本次發布均改依法定數計算預算達成率(實徵淨
額占全年預算數)。 
2.累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額占累計分配預算數 115.0%，占全年預算數
78.8%。 
圖 1 全國賦稅收入及年增率

說明：115 年 5-7 月年增率跳動較大，係因 114 年所得稅結算申報及繳納期限延長至 6 月底所致。

二、主要稅目別變動
（一）9 月實徵淨額與上年同月比較，以營業稅增加 297 億元、營利事業
所得稅增加 254 億元及證券交易稅增加 222 億元較多。
（二）累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額與上年同期比較，以證券交易稅增加 3,077
億元、營利事業所得稅增加 1,853 億元及營業稅增加 1,203 億元較多。
圖 2 主要稅目別變化
(115 年 1-9 月)
1.營利事業所得稅
(1)9 月實徵淨額 2,007 億元，較上年同月增加 254 億元(+14.5%)，主
因暫繳稅款成長。
(2)累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額 1 兆 28 億元，較上年同期增加 1,853 億元
(+22.7%)，主因結算申報自繳 、暫繳稅款 俱增及延分期稅款陸續
入帳。
2.綜合所得稅
9 月實徵淨額 490 億元，較上年同月增加 34 億元(+7.4%)，主因扣繳
稅款增加所致；累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額 8,279 億元，較上年同期增加
790 億元(+10.6%)，主因結算申報自繳增加，以及盈餘分配、薪資所

得等扣繳稅款均呈成長。
3.營業稅
9月實徵淨額1,414億元，較上年同月增加297億元(+26.6%)，累計1至
9月實徵淨額6,230億元，較上年同期增加1,203億元(+23.9%)，均因內
需穩定，加以電子零組件 、資通與視聽產品 等進口代徵稅額增加 ，
惟出口暢旺，適用外銷零稅率營業人申請退稅亦增，抵銷部分增幅。

4.貨物稅
(1)9月實徵淨額100億元，較上年同月減少11億元(-10.0%)，主因汽柴
油減徵幅度擴大，惟電動車大量到港推升進口 車稅額，抵銷部分
減幅。
(2)累計1至9月實徵淨額874億元，較上年同期減少207億元(-19.2%)，
除汽柴油降稅因素外 ，亦與新購汽機車及電動車之減免徵退稅增
加有關。
5.證券交易稅
(1)9 月及累計 1 至 9 月上市上櫃股票平均每日成交值為 1 兆 317 億
元、1 兆 1,461 億元，分別較上年同期(6,006 億元、4,451 億元)增
71.8%、1.6 倍。
(2)9 月 證 交 稅 實 徵 淨 額534 億 元， 較 上 年 同 月 增 加 222 億 元
(+71.2%)；累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額 5,060 億元，較上年同期增加
3,077 億元(+1.6 倍)。
6.關稅
9 月實徵淨額 157 億元，較上年同月增加 3 億元(+1.9%)，累計 1 至 9
月實徵淨額 1,224 億元，較上年同期增加 80 億元(+7.0%)，均因機械

及電機設備、基本金屬及其製品等進口稅額增加。
7.贈與稅
9 月實徵淨額 23 億元，較上年同月增加 6 億元(+35.7%)，累計 1 至 9
月實徵淨額 211 億元，較上年同期增加 43 億元(+25.3%)，主因大額
稅款案件增加。
8.土地增值稅
9 月實徵淨額 56 億元，較上年同月增加 12 億元(+26.6%)，主因大額
稅款案件增加；累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額 504 億元，較上年同期減少
14 億元(-2.8%)，主因房地交易量及大額稅款案件減少。
9.印花稅
9 月實徵淨額 31 億元，較上年同月增加 4 億元(+16.2%)，累計 1 至 9
月實徵淨額 175 億元，較上年同期增加 15 億元(+9.0%)，主因銀行放
款稅額成長。
（三）前 9月主要稅目實徵淨額占分配預算數之比率：
1.大於 100%者有證交稅(272%)、遺產稅(155%)、贈與稅(132%)、印花
稅(130%)、營業稅(116%)、關稅(114%)、契稅(111%)、房屋稅(106%)、

綜所稅(101%)、牌照稅(101%)及營所稅(101%)等。
2.低於 100%者有貨物稅(98%)、菸酒稅(97%)、土增稅(85%)、地價稅
(84%)及特銷稅(57%)等。

三、各級政府賦稅收入概況
(一)中央政府
累計 1 至 9 月中央政府賦稅收入 (不含中央統籌分配稅款 )實徵淨 額
2 兆 3,603 億元(占分配預算數 119.3%)，較上年同期增加 2,553 億元
(+12.1%)，雖新版財政收支劃分法 開始施行，營業稅劃歸中央政府 比
率大幅調降，減少 2,748 億元(-90.7%)，惟證交稅、所得稅分別增加 3,077
億元、2,230 億元，互抵後呈現上升。
(二)地方政府
累計 1 至 9 月地方政府賦稅收
入(含中央統籌分配稅款 )實徵
淨額 1 兆 898 億元(占分配預
算數 109.4%)，較上年同期增
加 4,527 億元(+71.0%)，主因
納入中央統籌分配 之營業稅
增加 4,030 億元(+2.1 倍)所致。
(三)中央特種基金
累計 1 至 9 月撥入中央特種基金之健康福利捐實徵淨額 195 億元，較
上年同期增加 2 億元(+0.8%)；房地合一課徵所得稅撥入住宅基金 135
億元，較上年同期減少 7 億元(-4.7%)；撥入長照基金 883 億元(其中 541

億元來自房地合一課徵所得稅、195 億元來自菸稅，146 億元來自遺贈
稅)，較上年同期增加 8 億元(+0.9%)。

### Page 2

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
⚫ 美國 9 月通膨數據高於市場預期，CPI 月增 0.6%、年增 3.7%；核心 CPI 月增
0.3%、年增 2.6%。同月 PPI 月增 1.3%，創 2022 年 3 月以來最大增幅，年增
6.3%， 顯示通膨壓力已逐步由能源價格擴散至更廣泛領域 。 數據公布後 ， 市場一
度提高對聯準會 10 月升息的預期，但隨後多位官員釋出審慎訊號，使升息押注
降溫 。 聯準會理事鮑曼表示 ， 決策前仍有時間觀察先前升息效果 ； 舊金山聯準銀
行總裁戴莉則認為 10 月無立即升息之必要。而褐皮書顯示，美國就業市場維持
「低招聘、低裁員」格局，而高能源價格帶來的通膨壓力仍值得關注。
⚫ 中國 9 月通膨持續回升，CPI 年增 0.9%，創近三個月高點；PPI 年增 5.0%，創
逾四年最大漲幅，顯示通縮壓力進一步減輕。主要受能源與原物料價格上漲帶
動，且 9 月進出口表現優於預期，AI 產業鏈需求與貿易環境改善提供支撐；不

過，信貸增長仍仰賴政府發債與季底效應，內需與民間融資動能仍偏弱。
⚫ 日本銀行審議委員小枝淳子表示 ， 不宜對升息過於保守 ， 並認為政策利率仍有進
一步上調空間。總裁植田和男則強調維持2%通膨目標的重要性。整體而言，日
本央行持續釋出偏鷹派訊號，有助支撐日圓表現。
⚫ 歐洲央行管委溫施表示 ， 在能源價格波動下 ，為了控制通膨仍不排除進一步升息
可能，總裁拉加德則認為法國債務問題應透過財政政策解決， 歐洲央行官員普遍
認為能源價格的潛在波動依然是推升通膨並使其居高不下的主要風險來源。。
⚫ 澳洲央行會議紀錄指出，能源價格上漲與需求韌性可能使通膨更具黏性，保留
進一步升息空間。然而 9 月就業數據意外疲弱，市場大幅下調升息預期，目前
預估 11 月維持利率不變的機率接近 90%。

### Page 3

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
美元兌新台幣匯率走勢
Oct/19/2026
＜上週走勢＞
上週台幣匯價高點為 31.890，低點在 32.520，以 32.338 作收，較前一周收盤價
貶值 4 角 6.3 分。
上週台幣走貶 ， 周初 ， 外資延續前周大幅匯出的走勢 ， 雖逢高吸引出口商拋匯 ，
仍不敵外資匯出力道令台幣走貶 ； 週二台股重挫近千點 ， 外資大幅賣超台股1653 億
並匯出，台幣接連跌破 32.000、32.100 及 32.200，總成交量 71.3 億，創歷史新高；
周三台股收漲，外資雙向進出，台幣收斂部分跌幅；週四，受美國通膨高於預期影
響，早盤台幣跳空開低，台股跌逾2%，外資大幅賣超 3025 億，台幣跌破 31.400 關
卡 ， 總成交量91.2 億 ， 再創歷史新高 ； 週五外資持續匯出 ， 台幣貶至周內低點32.520，
終場在央行調節之下收在 32.338。
＜未來分析＞
預估本週台幣走勢偏弱。就國際市場而言，美國 CPI、PPI 雙雙高於預期，通

膨壓力仍在，儘管聯準會官員陸續發言表示不急於升息，淡化市場對於聯準會十月
升息預期，惟年底前聯準會仍有升息一碼空間，利率持穩於高位將給予國際美元支
撐；就台灣市場來看，市場對AI 產業資本支出、融資成本及信用風險產生疑慮，若
賣壓延續，將壓抑市場風險偏好，增加外資調整台股部位及匯出的壓力，令台幣面
臨持續貶值的壓力，惟出口商拋匯將抑制台幣貶值速度，綜合以上，預估本週台幣
走勢偏弱。
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：32.100-32.700

### Page 4

4  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Oct/19/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣震盪走升。上週人民幣中間價連續走升，週三中間價升至6.7249 創
三年八個月新高，惟市場靜待美國公布通膨數據，交投謹慎，人民幣僅小幅走升；
週三晚間，美國公布 9 月 CPI 年增率 3.7%、核心 CPI 年增 2.6%，雙雙高於預期，
帶動美元指數升至年內高點 102.945、 人民幣貶至周內低點6.7005； 然多位聯準會官
員發言表示聯準會不急於升息，令市場對於聯準會十月升息預期略有降溫，美元指
數自高點回落，激勵人民幣由貶轉升；臨近周末，聯準會主席華許於IMF 會議發言
並未指引十月聯準會政策利率走向，再度淡化市場對於聯準會十月升息預期，國際
美元走勢偏弱，人民幣走升觸及年內高點 6.6825，終場境外人民幣收在 6.6850，週
線升值 91 點，境內人民幣則收在 6.6870，週線升值 60 點。

＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週人民幣震盪偏升。就國際市場來看，美國仍面臨高通膨、年底可能將
再次升息，利差因素支撐美元指數於高檔震盪，限縮人民幣升值空間；就中國國內
市場來看，中間價持續偏升將帶動人民幣走升，加以出口商實質拋匯需求將支撐人
民幣表現；最後就技術面來看，USD/CNH 日線圖中，匯價維持在五日均線之下交
投 ， 均線呈現空頭排列 ， 加以USD/CNH 有效跌破 6.6900 整數關卡 ， 顯示USD/CNH
空方勢力較盛，綜合以上，預估本週人民幣震盪偏升。

＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週 USD/JPY 先上後下。週初，受油價攀升推升美國通膨與利率預期帶動，美
元指數溫和走升創 6 月底以來新高，推升 USD/JPY 突破 158.50；週間，美國公布 9
月 CPI 高於預期，帶動美元指數升至 2025 年 5 月以來高點，也使 USD/JPY 最高來
到 158.94，然逢高獲利了結賣壓出籠使匯價回落；臨近週末，USD/JPY 一度下探至
157.53，隨後逢低買盤進場收復部分跌幅，終場收在 157.99，週線下跌 13 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、受美伊戰事推升汽柴油飆漲
與服務成本擴散影響，美國 9 月 CPI 及 PPI 月增漲幅均超出市場預期，加上初請失
業金人數連五週低於 20 萬人顯示勞動市場穩定，市場預期聯準會將於 12 月再次升
息，美日實質利差維持高檔，持續為USD/JPY 提供下檔支撐。二、被視為鴿派的日
本經濟財政大臣城內實明確表態，貨幣政策應交由日本央行自行判斷，並宣告日本
已擺脫長期通縮、轉型為增長導向經濟體。此番言論降低政治干預日銀進一步升息
的疑慮，在貨幣政策正常化的腳步下，預計將限縮 USD/JPY 的上行空間。三、就技
術面而言，日線圖中，USD/JPY 匯價目前交投於月線及年線之間，各天期均線高度
糾結，多空雙方於 158 關卡附近拉鋸，在有效突破季線及年線反壓或跌破月線支撐
前 ， 仍以區間震盪看待。 綜合以上 ， 預估USD/JPY 本週呈現區間震盪的可能性較高 。

歐元反彈空間。三、技術面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD 自低點反彈並站回五日線

一、 台幣貨幣市場部分 ，本週央行連續減發定存單使市場資金寬鬆 ， 雖外資大舉匯
出，資金供給仍充裕，帶動短率朝區間低位移動。

預期區間 1.60%-1.62%，較上期標售結果下跌 5.8 個基本點。 
週 五 隔 夜 拆 款 利 率 區 間 在 0.805%~0.830% ， 30 天 期 短 票 主 要 成 交 在
1.67%~1.68%，利率大致持平於上週。 
二、 5 年期指標券 A15108 週二搓合成交在 1.88%，後隨美債利率滑落，加上本週
2 年期公債標債結果提振市場持券信心，帶動 10 年期指標券 A15109 週四向
下成交在 2.02%~2.025%。 
在銀行去化短期資金及補充 LCR 之需求下 ， 週三2 年期公債得標利率為1.65%，
落在市場預估區間下緣 ， 投標倍數回升至2.86 倍 ， 銀行業得標比重上升至88%。
股市承壓帶動避險需求，美債利率大幅回檔，促使台債交投偏空氛圍轉趨和
緩 ， 惟在央行維持緊縮貨幣政策下 ， 買盤積極追價的意願不強 ， 預期短線台債
殖利率呈區間格局的可能性較高，關注下週 10 年券標售結果，預估 5 年券區
間為 1.85%~1.90%，10 年券區間為 1.98%~2.03%。

國際經濟金融週報內容摘要 
美 
國 
美國經濟維持溫和擴張，勞動市場降溫與通膨黏著牽動聯準會政策 
經濟 
⚫ 2026 年第 2 季美國實質 GDP 季增年率終值上修至 2.2%， 惟低於第1 季
之 2.5%。成長主要由個人消費及固定投資帶動，其中消費貢獻 2.51 個
百分點；惟進口大增 12.6%，致淨出口拖累 1.10 個百分點。另實質私人
國內最終銷售成長 4.6%，顯示內需仍具韌性。 
⚫ 9 月美國非農就業僅增 2.9 萬人，遠低於前月及近一年平均，失業率升
至 4.2%， 薪資增幅續降 ， 顯示企業聘僱動能轉弱 。 惟勞動市場尚未明顯
惡化，後續仍須關注就業降溫對聯準會利率決策之影響。 
⚫ 8 月美國職位空缺數由 733.5 萬個降至 707.9 萬個，職缺率降至 4.3%，
顯示企業徵才需求略為降溫；惟聘僱人數增至 519.2 萬人。主動離職及
裁員規模同步下降，反映勞工轉職意願與企業人力調整趨於審慎，勞動
市場供需大致維持平衡。 
⚫ 9 月美國 ISM 製造業採購經理人指數為 54.5，連續第九個月維持擴張。
新訂單、就業及未完成訂單同步改善，顯示需求仍具韌性；惟生產、出
口及進口動能略為放緩，且原物料成本持續攀升，加上關稅政策、地緣
政治及供應鏈不確定性仍存，製造業仍面臨部分壓力。 
⚫ 8 月美國 PCE 物價指數月增 0.3%、年增 3.4%，核心 PCE 月增 0.2%、
年增 3.0%， 顯示通膨大致持穩但短期物價壓力略升 。 另一方面 ， 個人消
費支出月增 0.9%、實質消費成長 0.6%，帶動儲蓄率降至 4.1%，顯示家
庭需求仍具韌性；後續須關注消費熱度及通膨對聯準會政策之影響。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 近期聯準會官員立場趨於分歧，理事 Michael Barr 認為能源價格及 AI
投資熱潮可能延緩通膨降溫 ， 未來仍可能進一步升息 ； 惟John Williams、
Philip Jefferson 及 Michelle Bowman 等官員則主張暫無急於升息的必要 ，
傾向觀察後續經濟數據。加以 9 月非農就業明顯放緩，市場對 10 月升
息預期大幅降溫，焦點轉向 12 月會議。 
⚫ 台積電傳評估與馬斯克旗下 Terafab 合作赴德州設廠，雙方仍處討論階
段。合作可能由台積電主導營運，或由 Terafab 持多數股權、台積電提
供技術 。 若成真 ， 將擴大台積電美國布局 ， 並支援Tesla、SpaceX 及 xAI
晶片需求。

兆豐國際商業銀行 徵信處 
115.10.5 
一、 美國—美國經濟維持溫和擴張，勞動市場降溫與通膨黏著牽動聯準會政策 
2026 年第二季美國實質 GDP 季增年率終值由前次估計之 1.5%上修至 2.2%，
惟仍低於第一季之 2.5%， 顯示經濟持續擴張但成長動能略有放緩 。 此次上修主要
反映投資、消費及政府支出優於原先估計。就成長結構觀察，個人消費支出仍為
主要動能，貢獻 2.51 個百分點，其中服務及商品消費分別貢獻 1.57 及 0.94 個百
分點；私人投資貢獻 0.82 個百分點，以設備投資及智慧財產權投資表現強勁，惟
庫存調整拖累 0.53 個百分點。對外貿易方面，出口成長 5.0%，但進口大幅增加
12.6%， 致淨出口拖累GDP 成長 1.10 個百分點 。 另反映內需基本面的實質私人國
內最終銷售成長 4.6%， 高於第一季之1.8%， 顯示民間需求明顯增強 。 物價方面 ，
第 2 季 PCE 及核心 PCE 物價指數分別上升 5.0%及 3.3%， 顯示通膨壓力仍偏高 。
整體而言，美國經濟仍由消費與固定投資支撐，惟進口快速增加及通膨黏著性，
仍為後續經濟前景的重要觀察重點。 
2026 年 9 月美國非農就業人數增加 2.9 萬人 ， 明顯低於上月修正後的13.3 萬
人，也低於過去 12 個月平均每月增加 4.5 萬人的水準；失業率小幅升至 4.2%，
整體仍維持在 3 月以來 4.1%至 4.3%的區間，反映勞動市場整體變化有限，惟新
增就業力道已轉趨疲弱。家庭調查方面，勞動參與率升至 61.8%，就業人口比為
59.2%，均較上月小幅上升；長期失業人數約 194.4 萬人，占總失業人口 27.1%，
大致維持穩定。產業別方面，主要產業就業變化有限，其中醫療保健增加 1.7 萬
人、營建業增加 1.1 萬人、製造業增加 0.9 萬人，金融活動則減少 0.7 萬人，政府
部門減少 1.7 萬人。薪資方面，私人部門平均時薪升至 37.81 美元，月增 0.1%、
年增 3.0%，平均每週工時維持 34.4 小時。另 7 月與 8 月新增就業合計下修 6 萬
人，反映前二月就業動能較初估疲弱。整體而言，9 月美國新增就業明顯放緩、
失業率小幅上升且薪資增速降溫，企業聘僱動能較先前轉弱；惟失業率仍維持於
近期區間，勞動市場尚未出現明顯惡化跡象，後續仍須關注企業聘僱需求是否持
續降溫，以及對聯準會利率決策的影響。 
2026 年 8 月美國職位空缺數(Job Openings) 由上月修正後之 733.5 萬個減少
25.6 萬個至 707.9 萬個 ， 職缺率由4.4%降至 4.3%， 顯示企業用人需求略有降溫 ；

2026 年 9 月美國 ISM 製造業採購經理人指數(Manufacturing PMI) 為 54.5，
較上月之 54.6 微降 0.1 點，惟已連續第九個月維持於 50 以上擴張區間，顯示製

產指數由 58.3 降至 56.7，雖仍維持擴張，但成長速度略有放緩；就業指數由51.2
升至 52.7，反映企業聘僱需求持續增加。供應商交貨指數由 59.3 微降至 59.0，交
貨延遲情況仍存；庫存指數則由 50.6 降至 48.6，企業備貨態度轉趨保守。此外，
價格指數由 71.1 升至 77.9，原物料及投入成本壓力升高；未完成訂單指數由51.8
升至 56.4，訂單去化變慢。另一方面，新出口訂單指數由53.2 降至 50.9，進口指
數亦由 52.5 降至 51.0，外需成長動能及企業備料需求略為放緩。整體而言，9 月
美國製造業活動持續穩健擴張，新訂單、就業及未完成訂單同步改善，顯示製造
業景氣仍具韌性；惟出口及進口增速放緩、投入成本壓力升高，加上關稅政策、
地緣政治風險及供應鏈不確定性仍存，後續發展仍值得持續關注。 
2026 年 8 月美國個人消費支出(PCE)物價指數月增率由上月之 0.1%升至 0.3%，
年增率維持於 3.4%； 扣除食品及能源後之核心PCE 月增率由 0.1%升至 0.2%，年
增率亦持平於 3.0%，顯示整體通膨大致持穩，惟短期物價漲勢較前月略有回升。
另一方面，個人所得月增 0.2%，可支配所得(DPI)月增 0.3%，實質可支配所得持
平；個人消費支出月增 0.9%，實質個人消費支出成長 0.6%，顯示民間消費表現
明顯回溫。由於消費增幅高於所得增幅，個人儲蓄率由前月之 4.6%降至 4.1%。
整體而言，8 月美國通膨表現大致持穩，但消費支出顯著回升，反映家庭需求仍
具韌性；後續仍須持續關注消費熱度、通膨走勢及其對聯準會貨幣政策路徑之影
響。

二、歐元區—法國殖利率大幅上升，不利於投資及消費表現 
 歐元區 9 月 HICP 通膨 、 核心通膨(不含食品及能源)分別上升 0.6、0.1 個百分
點至 3.8%、2.5%， 主因國際原油價格上漲 ， 使能源通膨上升4.5 個百分點至 18.8%，
並透過肥料、物流成本上漲，間接推升食品通膨 0.3 個百分點至 1.4%。另外，服
務通膨上升 0.2 個百分點至 3.2%，近三個月服務通膨大致呈現橫向波動，部分反
映旅遊旺季因素影響。基於布蘭特原油持續位於每桶 90 美元以上，預期年底前
仍將對通膨帶來額外壓力，後續須關注能源漲價可能會透過運輸、物流等成本管
道傳導至核心商品及服務價格，進而提高核心通膨黏性。

求持續增溫，將有助於穩定勞動市場表現；(2)歐元區第二季家庭儲蓄率下降 0.2

暫時不利於投資及消費信心。 
三、日本—景氣溫和復甦，但消費放緩與通膨升溫風險升高 
 8 月工業生產月減 1.7%，主要係受到該月地震及豪雨等天然災害影響，導致
部分工廠暫時停產。從細項來看，汽車業受停工影響最為明顯，產量下滑6.8%，
電子產品產量亦減少 1.9%。 惟生產機械產量連續三個月成長 ， 加上核心資本財出
貨持續增加，顯示企業投資需求仍相當強勁，有助於支撐後續工業生產回升。從
企業預期來看，製造商預期9 月產量將成長 3.2%，顯示生產活動可望迅速回溫，
在供應鏈逐步恢復、庫存回補需求持續，以及穩健的企業資本支出支撐下，預計
第三季工業生產仍可維持正成長，且成長動能可望延續至年底。 
 8 月零售月減 1.2%，雖然表現略優於市場預期，但仍顯示民間消費動能正在
放緩。此次疲弱主要原因來自耐久財銷售下滑，其中家電銷售因空調等產品在換
機需求告一段落後明顯回落，汽車銷售也隨政府刺激措施效應逐漸消退而連續第
四個月下降。相較之下，食品、服裝等非耐久財銷售雖同步走弱，但降幅相對有
限 ， 反映薪資成長仍對消費提供部分支撐 。惟不利天候與通膨壓力仍是主要風險 ，
8 月至 9 月持續豪雨已影響服裝等非必需消費，而除米價以外的食品及商品價格
持續上漲，也正侵蝕家庭實質購買力，恐限制消費者信心與消費復甦力道。 
 9 月東京核心通膨年增率升至 2.7%，雖然東京都免費托兒及免費水費政策造
成的基期效應貢獻約 0.5 個百分點，但通膨升溫主因仍是價格上漲已由能源與食
品擴散至整體經濟。受石油價格上揚帶動，企業開始加速將原物料及薪資成本轉
嫁給消費者，帶動商品與服務價格普遍走高；租金通膨更升至 2%，創 1994 年以
來新高，公共交通及醫療等項目也開始調漲。隨著東京的物價壓力逐步反映至全
國數據，日本央行進一步升息的可能性升高。

四、中國—為刺激內需、引導產業走向，人行漸近式放寬政策工具 
9 月官方製造業 PMI 上升 0.3 點至 50.1，為近三個月高點，新訂單、出口訂

業指數加速衰退、企業展望減弱，皆拖累 PMI 指數水準。此外，投入、產出物價
指數分別上升至 60.8、54，顯示成本壓力加劇，但售價調整速度依然較為遲緩，
不利於獲利表現。官方非製造業 PMI 上升 1.2 點至 50.2，其中建築業活動指數大
增 3.4 點至 50.3，脫離連續八個月的衰退區間，主要是政府加大財政刺激力度，
推動重大建設開工；非製造業的從業人員指數為 45.9，顯示就業情況仍顯嚴峻，
亦較不利於內需表現。 
鑒於內需動能疲弱，人行於 9 月 29 日宣布下調抵押補充貸款(該貸款本質是
將資金引導至政府希望發展的領域)利率自 1.75%降至 1.50%，並擴大適用範圍，
將水網、新型電網、算力網、新一代通信網、城市地下管網及物流網等建設納入
支持，有助於提振內需動能。未來若內需復甦力道不如預期，不排除人行將進一
步降準或擴大結構性貨幣工具支持經濟；至於全面降息則仍須兼顧人民幣匯率及
中美利差等因素，可能性相對較低。

## Document 126: 261016_daiwa_ Ardentec

Report source: line報告備份

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: We preview Ardentec’s 3Q26 results. In our initiation report, 
“Initiation: an emerging force in AI/HPC testing” (Sep 9), we expected 
traditional seasonality in 4Q26-1Q27 to weigh on Ardentec’s capacity 
utilisation. However, we now expect stronger-than-expected momentum in 
the networking segment to drive counter-seasonal resilience through 
1Q27E. We also expect AI revenue (with AI connectivity) contribution to 
exceed 40% in 1Q27E, well ahead of our original expectations.

What’s the impact: Robust networking demand bolsters high 
utilisation and CPO traction. Our industry checks indicate that demand 
for networking products is surging across the testing supply chain, primarily 
fuelled by the acceleration of 1.6T networking adoption. We forecast 
Ardentec’s revenue in 4Q26 and 1Q27 to beat typical seasonal slowdowns. 
As these high-speed networking programs largely follow a customer-
consignment model, their rising revenue mix should provide upside to 
overall gross margin. Concurrently, subsidiary Giga Solution is gaining 
momentum in co-packaged optics (CPO) testing, having already qualified 
and begun ramping up with leading US tier-1 players. While current 
engagement is centred on insertion 1, we see potential for customer base 
diversification and expansion into subsequent test insertions (2 and 3).

3Q26 results preview. With the Longtan fab expansion on track and 
spillover demand continuing, Ardentec 3Q26 revenue reached TWD5.34bn 
(+17.7% QoQ, +48.5% YoY), above the market consensus by 0.8% but 
slightly missing our estimate by 1.4%. With utilisation remaining high in 
3Q26, we estimate its gross margin and operating margin will reach 45.1% 
and 35.5%, respectively, above the consensus by 1.3pp and 1.9pp, 
respectively. We expect cleanroom construction of 2nd and 3rd floors will 
be finalised by year-end 2026 driven by solid domestic AI/HPC orders, with 
2nd-floor capacity likely contributing by late-1Q27.

What we recommend: We raise our 2027-28E EPS by 7% to reflect 
better-than-expected networking demand but lower our 2026E EPS by 1%. 
We reaffirm our Buy (1) rating and raise our 12M TP to TWD395 (from 
TWD340), based on an unchanged target PER of 25x on our 2027E EPS. 
While our target PER represents a premium over the OSAT/testing peer 
average, we believe this premium is justified by steady demand for high-
speed AI connectivity and the Longtan ramp-up. Our estimates indicate 
these factors could lift AI-related revenue to more than 40% of total sales 
by 1Q27. Key downside risk: delays in capacity expansion.

How we differ: Our 2027-28E EPS are 7-26% above the Bloomberg 
consensus, likely on our more aggressive utilisation rate and margin profile.

16 October 2026

3Q26 preview: AI defies seasonality

➢ Solid AI networking demand drives counter-seasonal resilience

➢ Reaffirming our Buy (1) rating; raising our 12M TP to TWD395

Company profile 
Founded in 1999, Ardentec is a leading Taiwanese pure-play wafer-testing provider serving foundries, 
IDMs and fabless chipmakers. Its acquisitions of Giga Solution and Raytek over 2016-17 added RF 
testing and bumping capabilities. Combining automotive-grade reliability with flexible AI/HPC capacity, 
the company provides customers a secondary supplier which enhances supply-chain resilience.

### Page 5

5

Ardentec (3264 TT): 16 October 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 9 Sep 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 2
The board demonstrates strong governance oversight, with 42% independent directors, regular
performance evaluations, and ESG-linked executive compensation. Governance quality is
further supported by a top 5% ranking in Taiwan’s corporate governance assessment for nine
consecutive years.
Capital management 2
Capital allocation appears disciplined, balancing expansion investments with shareholder
returns. The company funded major capacity growth while maintaining strong profitability and
distributed stable cash dividends, reflecting a prudent and shareholder-friendly capital
management approach.
Related party &
transaction 1
Related-party transactions appear well governed and transparent. The company states that
intercompany dealings follow arm’s-length principles and are not used for tax minimization, while
directors are required to disclose conflicts and abstain from related decisions.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Issue & News 
SK하이닉스  
(000660) 
 소음보다 방향성에 
류형근

hyungkeun.ryu@daishin.com

투자의견 BUY  
매수, 유지 
6개월 목표주가 3,200,000 
유지  
현재주가 
(26.09.23) 
1,862,000 
반도체업종

◼ 최근 시장은 2가지 Risk를 제기. 다만, 과도한 우려는 기우. 
◼ 현 주가는 보다 강인해지는 Fundamental의 가치를 미반영한 수준. 
◼ 비중 확대 전략 유효. 목표주가와 매수의견 유지.

투자전략: 업황을 대변하지 못하고 있는 주가 
목표주가와 매수의견 유지. 현 주가는 업황의 강세를 충분히 반영하지 못하
고 있다고 생각. 1) 메모리반도체 사이클의 추가 확장 (구매 경쟁 심화에 따른 
단기 가격 상승 폭 확대, 기대치를 상회할 4Q26 DRAM 판매량), 2) 3Q26 실
적발표에서 보다 구체화될 주주환원 정책의 방향성에 보다 집중할 필요. 
시장에서 제기하는 Risk가 있다면, 2가지. 1) 주요 고객사향 HBM4 품질 이슈, 
2) Solidigm IPO와 해외 공장 투자에 따른 경계심리 확대가 그 대상. 당사는 
해당 Risk가 Fundamental을 저해할 것으로 보고 있지 않으며, 단기 노이즈보
다는 사이클 확장의 선순환에 보다 주목할 필요가 있다고 생각. 
1. HBM4 품질 이슈, 과도한 우려는 기우 
3Q26부터 HBM4 출하가 본격화될 것이라는 기존 입장을 유지. HBM4 비중
은 3Q26 전체 판매량의 40%, 2027년 연간 50% 중반까지 상승할 것. 
일각에서는 주요 고객사향 HBM4 품질 이슈로 사업 성과가 기대치를 하회할 
수 있다는 우려를 표출. 다만, 과도한 우려는 기우. 후공정 단에서 문제가 일
부 발생했을 수 있으나, 해결 불가능한 Risk라 보기 어렵고, 불량에 따른 배
상금을 논할 단계도 아니라는 판단. 
2. 해외 공장 투자, 실보다 득이 크다 
언론보도에 따르면, SK하이닉스는 해외 공장 투자와 Solidigm IPO를 추진 중. 
해당 결정이 현실화될 경우, 시장은 단기적으로 이를 부정적으로 받아들일 
가능성이 존재. 다만, 결과적으로, 실보다 득이 큰 선택이 될 것이라 생각. 
- 해외 투자가 현실화되더라도, 실질 생산 기여 시점은 2028년 이후일 가능
성이 높음. 미국의 건설 경기와 공장 구축의 세부 단계 등을 감안할 필요. 
- 해외 투자 결정이 관세 Risk를 축소시킬 수 있는 변화라는 점도 감안할 필
요. “불확실성 해소”는 Upcycle의 중반부에서 반도체 주식에 대한 좋은 투자
포인트가 되어왔고, 이번에도 다르지 않을 것이라는 판단.

## Document 239: Asia week ahead_ BoJ rates, China’s Fifth Plenum, Taiwan and Korea data

Source PDF: all report pdf/Asia week ahead_ BoJ rates, China’s Fifth Plenum, Taiwan and Korea data.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-23
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

The Bank of Japan is expected to hold rates at 1.25%, while
Taiwan’s economy is set to record another quarter of double-
digit growth. Markets will also focus on China’s Fifth Plenum
meeting and South Korea’s third-quarter GDP data

Deepali Bhargava and Lynn Song
Asia Research highlights of the week
China’s GDP edges higher as activity stabilised in September
Japan: BoJ expected to hold rates at 1.25%
We expect the Bank of Japan to keep its policy rate unchanged at
1.25% on Friday as it assesses the impact of last month’s 25bp
hike. Our base case assumes two additional 25bp hikes in January
and April 2027, taking the policy rate to 1.75%. While inﬂation
remains elevated, policymakers may prefer more time to monitor
the economy, particularly as the government continues to support
domestic demand.

### Page 2

Japan will release its October Tokyo inﬂation data and September
retail sales and industrial production on Friday. Market consensus is
for headline inﬂation to remain unchanged at 2.7% year-on-year,
while core inﬂation eases slightly to 2.6%. Retail sales growth is
expected to accelerate to 3.3% YoY, while industrial production
rebounds by 1.3% month-on-month following August’s sharp
decline.
China: Fifth Plenum takes centre stage
The key event for China is the Fifth Plenary Session of the 20th

Chinese Communist Party Central Committee, taking place in
Beijing between 26-29 October. The focus will likely be political
rather than economic, but markets will scrutinise the communiqué
for new signals. China will also release September industrial proﬁts
data. We’ll be watching closely to see whether August’s slowdown
deepened or began to reverse.
Taiwan: Double-digit GDP growth expected
to continue
Taiwan will release third-quarter GDP data on Friday. We’re looking

for another quarter of double-digit growth, forecasting 11.4% YoY,
amid strong external demand. Strong retail sales growth over the
past few months suggests domestic demand will hold up as well.
South Korea: Growth expected to remain
resilient
South Korea releases third-quarter GDP data on Tuesday. Market
consensus expects growth to moderate to 3.2% YoY from 3.7% in
the second quarter, while sequential growth edges up to 0.8%
quarter-on-quarter. Continued strength in semiconductor demand

should support underlying momentum. A solid reading would
reinforce the Bank of Korea’s view that economic growth remains
resilient.

## Document 243: Australia and NZ Weekly 5 October 2026 (2026-10-05)

Date: 2026-10-05

### Page 1

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.

### Page 2

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
As was universally expected, the RBA Monetary Policy
Board (MPB) lifted the cash rate by 25bps to 4.6% at its
September 2026 meeting.
This is quite the turnaround from the 11 August meeting,
where an extended period on hold looked to be the base case.

At that time, the data had broken against the RBA’s more
hawkish view, and its published forecasts did not support
further hikes. But the MPB can now point to the emergence of
some of the upside risks to inflation it warned of in August.
The post-meeting statement was notable for its emphasis
on the Middle East conflict, noting that “global energy prices
are now much higher than had been assumed in the August
forecasts” – “much higher” is stronger language than one

normally sees in an RBA statement. The RBA’s liaison indicates
that firms are passing this on or planning to do so. The pass-
through was characterised as partial, and additional to
domestically driven inflation arising from capacity pressures. We
read this as implying that the RBA is concerned about upside
risks to underlying inflation in Q4 of this year, as well as Q3.
The RBA is also wary about pressure on both construction
costs and retail prices of IT goods arising from the AI/data

centre boom, although in the media conference, the Governor
clarified that risks there were building rather than materialising.
The broader background highlighted in the Governor’s opening
statement to the media conference, is of demand outstripping
supply. The statement evidenced this by noting the stronger
July CPI and June quarter GDP results than expected.
The accompanying statement was notable for the absence of
language about the labour market being a little tight, despite

this being the inter-meeting message from the Governor
and other staff (see, for example, the Governor’s opening
statement on 18 September) and the language in the post-
meeting media conference. Views clearly differ on this: we
note that the Treasury forecasts underlying the May budget
and the recent Intergenerational Review imply both more
spare capacity in the labour market (a lower NAIRU) and faster
growth in potential from wherever the starting point is (higher

productivity growth and trend increase in participation).
Instead, the post-meeting statement characterised the
labour market as easing “broadly as expected”, despite the
unemployment rate already rising higher than the August SMP
forecast of 4.5% for the December quarter.
RBA hikes in September, November follow-up now expected
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
The decision was unanimous, suggesting that any qualms
external MPB members might have had about the RBA’s

downbeat view of supply capacity were overruled by resurgent
oil prices and the stronger than forecast CPI and GDP data.
With upside risks being seen to have emerged, the post-meeting
statement language included that the Board “will continue to
do what it considers necessary to bring inflation sustainably
back to target, including increasing the cash rate target further if
needed”, a small but meaningful change from the “if upside risks
materialise” language from the previous meeting, and back to

the tone of the language at the June meeting.
The bar for a follow-up hike in November is low. Indeed,
judging by the post-meeting rhetoric, a November hike is now
the base case, absent a lasting resolution of the Middle East
conflict beforehand, or some other event that significantly
lowers the outlook for energy-related costs in Australia. The
bar for hikes beyond that is much higher, given the cumulative
rise in interest rates, and noting that the labour market is

easing and the housing market will likely weaken further.
The August inflation data released the day after the meeting
was in line with our expectations. We therefore continue to
expect Q3 trimmed mean inflation around 0.9%qtr, too high for
the RBA’s comfort, and upside risks for Q4. Likewise, there are
no implications for our rates view from this week’s inflation data.
Forecast changes
• We have updated our financial forecasts to reflect the change

in our rate view.
• Our labour market forecasts are unchanged on the first
decimal place, but there is some modest upside risk to our
unemployment forecast.
• Our updated GDP profile can be found on page 9.

### Page 3

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: searching for signal
led the correction to date, the fact that the other capitals
are also reporting price declines clearly indicates that the
correction is deepening and broadening, as higher interest
rates collide with the tax policy changes announced in May’s

budget. Sentiment effects indeed remain at play, as evinced
by the continued pull-back in investor credit growth . Westpac
anticipates a peak-to-trough decline of around 7%, with tight
on-market supply limiting the extent of the correction. For
more, see our latest Housing Pulse.
Offshore, there was plenty of data to dissect.
In the US, inflation eased in August despite ongoing strength
in household demand and higher energy prices. Headline PCE
inflation rose 0.3%mth and 3.4%yr, while core PCE increased

0.2%mth and 3.0%yr. Encouragingly for the FOMC, the
share of PCE components rising by more than 3% over the
past year, a measure frequently cited by Fed Chairman Kevin
Warsh, declined to 45% from 49%, suggesting some easing in
broad-based inflation pressures. At the same time, household
spending remained robust, increasing 0.9%mth, equivalent
to around 0.6%mth in real terms. That said, personal income
growth was soft, rising 0.2% in the month, signalling

households opted to draw down savings.
The ISM manufacturing survey was stronger than the
headline suggested. While the index edged down 0.1pt to
54.5, manufacturing has now expanded for nine consecutive
months. There were some signs of capacity constraints
emerging, with production softened in the month but backlogs
up 4.6pts to 56.4, suggesting that firms struggled to keep
pace with demand. New orders rose 1.6pts to 55.3, primarily
due to domestic demand as new export orders eased, backlogs

increased. Firms responded by lifting employment, 1.5pts to
52.7. Against this backdrop manufacturers saw a renewed
acceleration in cost pressures, with the prices paid index
jumping 6.8pts to 77.9. Firms cited higher steel and aluminium
prices, elevated energy costs, rising freight charges and
ongoing semiconductor shortages, with every major industry
reporting higher input costs during the month.
The past week’s data will have done nothing to bridge the

clear divide across the FOMC, as evident in this week’s busy
Fedspeak. Some participants argued for further rate hikes,
citing strong inflation, diminished supply capacity and policy
that is not yet restrictive. Others counselled patience, pointing
to progress on inflation and that September’s hike buys time
to assess upcoming data. The one thing both agree on is that
inflation will be the primary determinant of the timing and
extent of further rate hikes. Our view remains that one further

hike is likely this cycle to bring policy closer to neutral.
In Australia, this week’s main event was the RBA Monetary
Policy Board’s (MPB) decision to raise the cash rate by 25bps
to 4.60%. This outcome was universally expected given
Governor Bullock had already communicated that upside risks
to inflation had materialised – the conditionality established
by the MPB back in August for considering another rate hike.
Governor Bullock made explicit note of three upside risks.

The Middle East conflict was emphasised as the risk that had
“materialised”, given the re-escalation and renewed surge in crude
oil and refined product prices over recent months. The RBA’s
liaison warns this could lead to further pass-through of costs
and more persistent inflation pressures. On top of this, domestic
capacity pressures and the data centre/AI investment boom were
also noted as upside risks that the MPB remain alert to.
In a video update mid-week, Chief Economist Luci Ellis

explains why the bar for a follow-up rate hike in November is
low. We now expect a 25bp increase in November, provided
there is no sudden resolution of the Middle East conflict
beforehand, or some other event that significantly lowers the
outlook for energy-related costs.
Subsequently, the August CPI revealed that underlying
(trimmed mean) inflation rose 0.2% (3.4%yr), which was
slightly below the market’s expectation but spot on Westpac’s
forecast. As usual, the monthly detail was mixed, but the key

drivers of underlying inflation remain centred on housing-
related components, particularly rents and new dwelling
purchases. Headline inflation remained volatile, with the
unwind of the fuel excise cut causing another spike in fuel
prices. We have maintained our forecast for a 0.9% lift in
trimmed mean inflation in Q3, and with upside risks building
for Q4, a follow-up rate hike in November appears most likely.
Regarding the current growth pulse, household spending

surprised with a flat reading in August. Services spend fell back
following recent strength that was driven by major sporting
events, but this was offset by an increase in goods spend,
particularly in fuel and electric vehicles. Together with our
latest card data for early September, the consumer spending
pulse is shading a touch softer. However, another solid
outcome for business investment could once again be on the
cards for Q3. August’s goods trade data showcased another

significant spike in data centre-related equipment imports, up
$4.3bn, similar in scale to Q1. While clearly import-driven, our
modelling suggests that the local economic impact of such
investment is likely to be around half of the total investment.
Before moving offshore, a final note on housing. Cotality’s
latest update revealed that dwelling prices fell another 1.1%
nationally in September. While Sydney and Melbourne have
Sian Fenner, Head of Business and Industry Economics

4
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
In China, policymakers announced another round of targeted
support measures aimed at stabilising growth and supporting
the struggling property sector. The PBOC lowered rates on its
Pledged Supplementary Lending facility by 25bps to 1.5% and
expanded quotas for targeted lending programs. Authorities

also introduced subsidies for eligible first-home buyers. The
subsidy applies to homes with a floor area of 120m² or less
and a value below CNY1.5mn, with support capped at 1ppt of
mortgage costs, up to CNY10,000 and for a maximum term of
five years. The measures complement reforms announced in
August to improve developer financing conditions and support
housing completion. However, weak credit demand and
confidence remains a significant constraint, suggesting policy

transmission is still limited.
The September PMI data pointed to a modest improvement
in activity, although the details were less convincing.
Manufacturing returned to expansionary territory, rising 0.9pts
to 50.1, supported by stronger production and new orders.
Much of the improvement, however, reflected a rebound in
prices, with the raw materials price index rising 7.6pts and
producer prices increasing 6.2pts. Labour market conditions
remained weak, with the employment index falling a further

0.6pts and remaining below 50 for almost four years. Conditions
in the non-manufacturing sector also improved, with the
services PMI lifting from 49.0 to 50.2, driven largely by stronger
construction activity. Input and output prices moved higher,
selling prices returned back above 50, export orders improved
to 48.5 and employment increased 0.5pts, although at 45.9 it
remains firmly in contractionary territory. Overall, the surveys
suggest activity is stabilising and policy support is gaining some

traction, but weak labour demand, subdued credit growth and
soft domestic demand indicate the recovery remains fragile.
In Japan, the September Tankan survey delivered another
constructive signal on the economy and reinforced the case
for further policy normalisation. Business conditions improved
by 2pts for large manufacturers and 5pts more broadly, while
profitability indicators strengthened and capital expenditure
intentions remained elevated. Large manufacturers expect

fixed investment to increase by 11.6% in FY2026, while large
non-manufacturers expect investment growth of 11.2%,
pointing to continued confidence in the medium-term outlook.
Capacity indicators improved further across both
manufacturing and non-manufacturing sectors, with measures
of excess production capacity becoming less negative
and indicating that demand remains sufficiently strong to
keep pressure on existing capacity. Labour shortages also

intensified, with employment conditions becoming more
negative across most industries and firm sizes, pointing to a
progressively tighter labour market. Inflation expectations
remained anchored around 2%, providing further evidence
that Japan is moving towards a more sustainable inflation
environment. Overall, the survey was consistent with an
economy operating close to capacity and supports the view
that a December rate hike remains a realistic possibility.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
Resilient activity and simmering inflation
While bumpy and uneven, New Zealand’s economic
recovery has continued over the past few months, with
activity showing resilience in the face of continued global
headwinds. Under the surface, however, inflation pressures

are bubbling away, with much of that pressure centred on
parts of the economy that aren’t sensitive to interest rates.
Economic activity getting back on firmer footing
Looking first at recent activity data, the economy has been
finding its feet again after taking a knock in the wake of the
Middle East war. That has been seen most clearly in the jobs
market, with the number of filled jobs rising by 0.9% in the
year to August. While not shooting the lights out, that is still a

noticeable improvement from the early part of the year, when
filled job numbers were falling.
Under the surface, it’s still a very mixed picture. Agriculture
remains the main engine of growth, with the strength in
commodity export earnings helping to boost incomes,
confidence and spending in regions with strong rural backbones.
That’s supported firm job growth in regions like Canterbury
(where filled job numbers are up 1.9% over the past year), Otago

(+1.5%) and Southland (+3.1%). In contrast, jobs growth remains
much more moderate in Auckland (up 0.6%) and Wellington
(up just 0.4%). That town-and-country split is likely to remain a
feature of the economic landscape for some time yet.
Looking ahead, businesses expect trading conditions to
remain resilient through the final months of the year. That
was reflected in September’s Business Outlook survey.
While the survey’s gauges of expected business activity and

general business confidence eased over the past month, they
remain at firm levels, consistent with continued moderate
growth through the latter part of the year. That resilience has
also been evident in the household sector, with consumer
confidence largely steady through September.
The resilience in household and business sentiment is notable,
as fuel prices have taken a big step higher over the past month.
The average price of 91 unleaded has risen by around 49c/litre –

albeit late in the month and likely not fully reflected in surveyed
confidence – taking it back to the levels seen during the early
stages of the Middle East conflict. Those higher fuel prices
have taken a bite out of household disposable incomes, with
spending on fuel up about 22% compared with this time last
year, even though the volume of fuel sold is down around 11%.
But even with the lift in fuel prices, we haven’t seen the same
degree of pessimism as earlier in the year, when fuel prices first

spiked. Notably, those higher fuel prices haven’t spilled over
into a broader lift in output price pressures either (at least at
this stage). Inflation expectations were steady in September’s
household and business surveys. Similarly, the number of firms
planning to raise prices fell back despite a lift in wage and cost
expectations. However, while that stability in pricing gauges is
welcome, measures of inflation pressure remain elevated.
Taken together, recent data reinforce indications that the

economy is shaking off the impacts of the Iran war.
Persistent inflation drivers and the RBNZ
Turning to inflation, the spike in oil prices earlier this year saw
annual inflation climb to 4.1% in the year to June. With local
fuel costs rising again in recent weeks, we continue to expect
inflation to remain above 3% until at least mid-2027.
However, even before the recent rise in fuel costs, inflation had
been running hot for an extended period. Excluding the rise

in food and fuel prices, inflation in the year to June was 2.9%,
and it’s been running close to or above 3% for five years now.
As we discuss in our recent report, much of the strength in
inflation over the past year has been related to administered
costs, such as local council rates, or to sectors that are less
exposed to competitive pressures, such as electricity and
insurance. Those increases have persisted for an extended period,
and we expect further sizeable increases in many of these costs.

This ongoing firmness is a key reason why we expect domestic
(non-tradables) inflation to be higher than the RBNZ expects
over the longer term. It’s also part of the reason why we
expect the RBNZ will ultimately need to raise the OCR by
more than it assumed in its September policy statement.
We are forecasting a 25bp rate rise at the RBNZ’s December
meeting and expect the OCR to peak at 4% next year.
This is a tough environment for the RBNZ. With ongoing

increases in administered prices boosting core inflation at a
time when energy prices are driving headline inflation well
above the target range, it’s hard for the RBNZ to look through
them. The RBNZ had little headroom to absorb upside inflation
surprises, even before the recent spike in oil prices.
Satish Ranchhod
Senior Economist

### Page 6

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
6
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
But getting inflation back to target in the face of an oil price
spike and high administered prices will not be easy and involves
some tough trade-offs. Administered prices tend to be less
responsive to changes in economic conditions. As a result,
interest rate hikes are likely to have a bigger impact on parts of

the economy that are not the main drivers of inflation. Tighter
policy will also weigh on the labour market and near-term
growth, while increasing households’ debt-servicing costs.
This is an uncomfortable but necessary trade-off. Looking
through, or not responding to, these sorts of large and persistent
cost increases would still leave households facing significant
increases in overall living costs. In the long run, it would be
more damaging for households and the economy if generalised

inflation were to become embedded in price setting behaviour.
The Kiwi has lost its wings
Adding to near-term inflation risks has been the drop in the NZ
dollar. On a trade-weighted basis, the Kiwi has fallen around
4% since the start of September, and it’s begun the December
quarter around 4% below the level the RBNZ assumed in its
September policy statement.
The slide in the Kiwi is mainly due to the strength of the US
dollar, with NZD/USD down nearly 5% since the start of

September to sit just below 0.56 at the time of writing. Moves
against other currencies have been more modest. For example,
NZD/AUD is down 2% over the same period.
Technically, NZD/USD seems oversold. In the short term,
trends in the US dollar will dominate, including any response
to the US labour market report due on Friday evening. Any
turnaround in the USD would likely see NZD/USD trade back
towards the top of the 0.5580–0.5680 range that is governing

the pair for the time being.
NZD/AUD will continue to be driven by changing views on
interest rate differentials between the RBNZ and RBA. This
week, NZD/AUD corrected higher from around 0.8070 to as high
as 0.8110 as RBA cash rate expectations were repriced following
the RBA Board meeting and Australian CPI. In the short term, the
bias for NZD/AUD could be lower, given our view that the RBNZ
will pause at its late-October meeting and that the market is

under-pricing the risk of an RBA rate hike in November.
Next week’s QSBO, due on Tuesday, will be the key event
risk, with the inflation and growth gauges closely watched for
signs that might prompt the RBNZ to pause or hike. Markets
had previously priced in a high chance of a hike at the RBNZ’s
October meeting, but in recent days appear to be reassessing
that view.  Pricing for a 25bp hike in New Zealand could be
pared back towards a 50:50 call. The 0.8030–0.8150 range

is likely to contain the pair in the immediate period ahead.
Our medium-term view is for NZD/AUD to rise as the RBNZ
increases the OCR to 4% by the second half of 2027. We’ll
update our views on the NZD and other currencies in our next
Market Outlook, out Wednesday 14 October.
Fiscal update
The big uncertainty on the domestic horizon is, of course,
November’s general election, and at this stage the polling
looks tight. Neither the established left nor right bloc has a

clear majority.
Ahead of the election, the Treasury has released its Pre-election
Economic and Fiscal Update (PREFU), updating the Budget
2026 (BEFU) fiscal forecasts to reflect the latest economic and
fiscal information and a revised economic outlook. This allows
the public to assess the state of the Government’s finances. It
also provides other parties with a baseline against which to
calibrate their spending and taxation policies.
The PREFU revealed a stronger starting point for the

Government’s finances than had been expected in the Budget.
• A deficit of $8.6bn is estimated for 2025/26, down from the
Budget estimate of $11.9bn.
• Similarly, the forecast deficit for 2026/27 has been revised
down significantly to $6.8bn from $11.4bn.
• Thereafter, the OBEGALx deficit is expected to continue
declining gradually, moving to a surplus of $4bn in 2028/29.
This broadly follows the Budget profile, but is stronger
across the forecast period.

• Net core Crown debt is forecast to peak at 43.9% of GDP in
2027/28, below the 46.1% peak forecast in the BEFU. It’s then
forecast to decline to 39.5% of GDP by the end of the forecast
period in 2030/31, in line with the Government’s goal of
reducing debt below 40% of GDP over the medium term.
Given the improved outlook, NZDM announced a $4bn
reduction in the 2026/27 bond programme to $30bn and a
$15bn reduction in the forecast programme across the whole

period through to 2029/30.
The medium-term forecasts in the PREFU should be treated
with caution. The economic forecasts underpinning the
PREFU were finalised on 21 August, when both oil prices
and, especially, bond yields were materially lower than they
are today. Had the forecasts been finalised more recently, the
fiscal projections presented today would have been weaker,
at least in the near term. Indeed, the Treasury is upfront in
acknowledging that, while the risks are two-sided, the balance

is skewed to the downside.
Aside from the obvious uncertainty surrounding the economic
outlook because of unpredictable developments in the Middle
East, the approaching General Election, and the possibility of
a change in the composition of the Government, means the
assumptions underpinning the PREFU forecasts could quickly
become invalidated.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUS: Oct Westpac-MI Consumer Sentiment (index)
Oct 6, Last: 84.4
Consumer sentiment declined 5.2% to 84.4 in September,
taking the index back towards the deeply pessimistic levels seen
earlier in the year. Rising fuel prices and fears of an imminent

interest rate rise drove the move with a continued weakening in
housing markets likely adding to unease in some quarters.
Developments over the last month would have added to these
concerns with: the RBA raising the cash rate by 25bps to 4.6%
during the survey week; average pump prices pushing above
$2.36/litre nationally and no end in sight to global supply
disruptions;  and more weak updates on the housing market.
NZ: Q2 NZIER Survey of Business Opinion (index)

Oct 6, General business confidence last: 12.3
Business conditions picked up modestly in the June quarter as
concerns about the war in the Middle East eased. However,
cost and pricing gauges remained firm. The September quarter
survey will be closely watched ahead of the RBNZ’s 28
October meeting. Recent weeks have seen signs that activity
is firming. However, fuel prices have also risen sharply, and we
(along with the RBNZ) will be watching for signs of a broader

lift in inflation pressures. The timing of survey responses
within the sample period will also warrant scrutiny, given that
oil prices rose late in the quarter.

## Document 268: CoreWeave融资缺口有多大？高盛：只有230亿，买入！伯恩斯坦：760亿，危险！

Source PDF: all report pdf/CoreWeave融资缺口有多大？高盛：只有230亿，买入！伯恩斯坦：760亿，危险！.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-23
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

伯恩斯坦直指⾼盛模型存在乐观假设，⽽⾼盛⾃⾝现⾦流表却显⽰未来两年新增债务达 466
亿美元 —— 是其标题数字 230 亿美元的两倍。更关键的是，⾼盛同时持有 CoreWeave 逾 1%
股份并担任其承销商。当前 CoreWeave ⽆担保债券收益率已达 15% ，⾸笔 DDTL 还款恰在
此刻到期，亏损预计延续⾄ 2029 年。
摘要：
围绕同⼀家公司的融资缺⼝，华尔街两⼤机构给出了相差逾三倍的答案，将 AI 算⼒租赁商
CoreWeave 再度推⾄市场争议的⻛⼝浪尖。
伯恩斯坦分析师 Madison Rezaei 周四（ 10 ⽉ 22 ⽇）发布报告，将 CoreWeave ⽬标价从 74 美元下
调⾄ 51 美元，并估算该公司仅为履⾏现有合同承诺就需筹集 850 亿美元，扣除已有融资安排后，
净融资缺⼝⾼达 760 亿美元。这与⾼盛三天前给出的 230 亿美元估算相差超过三倍。
CoreWeave 股价周四再跌约 10% ⾄ 55.70 美元，抹去⾼盛升级评级后的全部涨幅。
⾼盛分析师 Gabriela Borges 周⼀将 CoreWeave 评级上调⾄ “ 买⼊ ” ，并于周三发布后续报告，将

融资缺⼝定性为 “ 约 230 亿美元 ”。然⽽，伯恩斯坦的报告随即指出⾼盛模型存在多处乐观假设，
且颇具讽刺意味的是，⾼盛⾃⾝的现⾦流模型所隐含的新增债务规模，实际上与伯恩斯坦的估
算更为接近。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

与此同时，⾼盛在报告披露中承认，其正担任 CoreWeave ⼀项待定承销业务的管理⼈或联席管
理⼈，并持有该公司逾 1% 的股份。
伯恩斯坦： 850 亿缺⼝，亏损延续⾄ 2029 年
伯恩斯坦的报告标题直⽩犀利 ——“Core-We've got bills to pay” （我们有账单要付）。分析师
Rezaei 在报告中写道， CoreWeave需要筹集 850 亿美元以履⾏现有已承诺积压订单，其中 90 亿
美元已由现有融资安排覆盖，净缺⼝为 760 亿美元。这 760 亿美元是在不签署任何新合同的前提
下，仅为交付已售出算⼒所需的资⾦。
伯恩斯坦同时否定了 “ 放慢增⻓、收割现⾦ ” 的退路。报告明确指出，即便 CoreWeave 停⽌争取
新合同，仍需为现有合同中嵌⼊的部署义务以及维护和替换需求提供资⾦， " ⽆新增积压 " 情景并
不会⽴即改善盈利状况。
在盈利预测上，伯恩斯坦⼤幅下调：2026 年调整后每股亏损从 4.56 美元扩⼤⾄ 5.14 美元， 2027
年从 4.77 美元扩⼤⾄ 6.81 美元，调整后亏损将⾄少持续⾄ 2029 年。

资本开⽀⽅⾯，伯恩斯坦预计 2027 年增⻓性资本开⽀约 460 亿美元，加上约 60 亿美元维护性资
本开⽀，合计 525 亿美元，⾼于市场⼀致预期；即便在 " ⽆新增积压 " 情景下， 2027 年资本开⽀仍
⾼达 435 亿美元。
⾼盛的 230 亿：三个关键假设撑起的数字
⾼盛的 230 亿美元结论建⽴在⼀套层层叠加的乐观假设之上。
华尔街⻅闻⽂章写道， Borges 的测算框架显⽰， CoreWeave 履⾏ 1290 亿美元积压订单约需 600
亿美元资本开⽀，其中 80% 可通过 DDTL （延迟提款定期贷款）进⾏债务融资，再扣除 106 亿美
元已承诺但未提取的 DDTL 额度及 104 亿美元 “ 现⾦期权 ” ，得出约 367 亿美元新增债务需求；进⼀
步假设客⼾预付合同总价值的 20% （约 157 亿美元），最终得出 229 亿美元的净融资缺⼝。

### Page 3

伯恩斯坦对此逐⼀提出质疑：
其⼀，预付款趋势背道⽽驰。⾼盛⾃⾝承认， CoreWeave 的客⼾预付款⽐例在过去六个⽉已经
下降。⾼盛的解释是，当债务融资能⻅度降低时，客⼾反⽽会更愿意预付 —— 但伯恩斯坦认
为，当客⼾看到 CoreWeave 的债券以 15% 以上的收益率交易时，未必愿意向⼀家债务处于困境
⽔平的交易对⼿预付资⾦。
其⼆， " 现⾦期权 " 过于乐观。⾼盛 104 亿美元的 " 现⾦期权 " 中包含以 64 美元价格出售 3500 万股
ATM 股票所得的 22 亿美元，⽽该股票当前交易价格约低于这⼀⽔平 9% 。此外，⾼盛假设可动⽤
20 亿美元循环信贷额度，但据伯恩斯坦的债务数据， 25 亿美元循环信贷中已有 12 亿美元被提
取，实际可⽤余额仅约 13 亿美元。
其三，最⼤情景下的数字被刻意淡化。⾼盛情景三（覆盖 4.2GW 电⼒容量）假设客⼾预付 527 亿
美元，若⽆此预付，所需新增债务将⾼达 845 亿美元，债务与贷款⽐率达 94% ，远超⾼盛⾃⾝设
定的 DDTL 结构 80% 上限。这⼀数字并未出现在报告标题中。

其四，报告全程回避资⾦成本。⾼盛计算了 CoreWeave 需要借多少钱，却未说明借款利率，仅
援引此前升级报告中 10% 的新增债务成本假设 —— ⽽ CoreWeave ⽆担保债券当前收益率约为
15% ，前者约为后者的三分之⼆。
⾼盛模型 " ⾃打嘴巴 "
伯恩斯坦报告中最具杀伤⼒的论据，或许来⾃⾼盛⾃⾝的财务模型。
翻阅⾼盛报告第⼆⻚的现⾦流量表，⾼盛预测 CoreWeave 2026 年⾃由现⾦流消耗 282 亿美元，
2027 年再消耗 282 亿美元， 2028 年消耗 158 亿美元；为此，⾼盛模型中的债务规模今年增加 267
亿美元， 2027 年增加 266 亿美元， 2028 年增加 200 亿美元 ——未来两年新增债务合计 466 亿美
元，是其报告标题 " 融资缺⼝ " 的两倍，且这还不包括⾼盛预测到 2028 年将达 500 亿美元的租赁负
债（实质上也是债务）。
按⾼盛⾃⾝模型， CoreWeave ⻓期债务将从 2025 年底的 149 亿美元增⾄ 2028 年的 908 亿美元；

2027 年净债务与股权之⽐峰值达 3059%； 2027 年底账⾯现⾦仅剩 12 亿美元，⽽届时年利息⽀出
⾼达 60 亿美元；利息覆盖倍数今年仅为 0.3 倍， 2027 年为 0.7 倍，直⾄ 2028 年才勉强升⾄ 1.3 倍。

### Page 4

DDTL 到期压⼒：账单已经开始到期
伯恩斯坦的报告还专⻔梳理了 CoreWeave 的债务结构与近期偿债压⼒。截⾄⽬前， CoreWeave
未偿债务总额 421 亿美元，年利息⽀出 33 亿美元，综合利率 7.8%—— 这⼀数字因 108 亿美元利率
仅 2%-3% 的可转换债券⽽被⼤幅拉低，其余债务均以垃圾级利率定价。
按伯恩斯坦的 DDTL 还款⽇历， DDTL 5.0 的⽉度还款已于约 2026 年 10 ⽉ 22 ⽇（即当前时点）启
动，⾸个 1.35 倍债务偿还覆盖率（ DSCR ）契约测试将于 11 ⽉ 30 ⽇前后到来； DDTL 5.5 将于明
年 1 ⽉开始摊还；规模达 85 亿美元的 DDTL 4.0 则将于明年 7 ⽉启动摊还。
在新增融资成本⽅⾯， CoreWeave ⽆担保债券收益率约 15% ，最新⼀笔有担保融资 DDTL 5.5
（今年 8 ⽉定价）的利差为 SOFR+5.50% ，是⾃ 2023 年⾸笔 DDTL 以来最宽的利差⽔平。伯恩斯
坦认为，后续融资的定价可能还会更⾼。

### Page 5

这构成了伯恩斯坦核⼼论点中的 “ 两难困境 ” ：CoreWeave 为保持竞争⼒需要转向与企业客⼾签
订更短期合同，短期合同单位定价更⾼，但对债权⼈⻛险更⼤，⽆助于降低融资成本。战略上
越正确的选择，融资成本越⾼。
9% 的⽣意， 32% 的故事
伯恩斯坦对 CoreWeave 最优质合同 —— 与 Meta 的协议（含 210 亿美元扩展） —— 进⾏了回报率
测算，得出最佳情景下⽆杠杆内部收益率（ IRR ）为 9%-12% （基准模型为 9% ）。加杠杆后 IRR
跳升⾄ 32% ，但这⼀数字的构成颇为脆弱：
CoreWeave 投⼊ 108 亿美元资本开⽀，借⼊其中 100 亿美元，⾃⾝仅投⼊ 7.56 亿美元股权，每年
获得 1.29 亿美元有杠杆⾃由现⾦流，32% 的回报率⼏乎完全依赖⾼杠杆以及对 2031 年六年旧
GPU 残值的押注。⽽这 9% 的⽆杠杆回报，恰好与有担保新增融资成本（约 9% ）持平，远低于
⽆担保融资成本（ 15% ）。 HSBC 此前以 8.9% 的投资资本回报率（ ROIC ）得出了相同结论。

### Page 6

管理层表⽰， 2024 年第⼆季度以来签署的合同 " 经济条款明显改善 " 。伯恩斯坦的回应是，新合同
规模可能太⼩，不⾜以显著改变整体混合回报率，其判断倾向于中期经济效益将更接近旧合
同。
英伟达是 " 地板 " ，但不是解药
伯恩斯坦并未预测 CoreWeave 归零。报告指出，该公司仍拥有庞⼤积压订单和已通电机房的交
付能⻅度，现有合同虽不够吸引⼈，但尚未亏损。
真正的底部⽀撑来⾃英伟达。伯恩斯坦认为，若 CoreWeave 陷⼊真正的下⾏困境，英伟达会介
⼊， “ 不⼀定能重振股价，但会抬⾼底部 ” 。这对 CoreWeave 股东是好消息，但对英伟达 CEO ⻩
仁勋⽽⾔略显尴尬 —— 他上周刚刚公开表⽰ CoreWeave 的资⾦成本 “ 实际上正在下降 ” ，⽽现实
数据显然并不⽀持这⼀说法。
伯恩斯坦同时认为， CoreWeave 的潜在冲击 “ 在⼀定程度上是可控的 ”—— 若其明天消失，托管
合同会被市场吸收，其余市场将继续运转。但报告也指出， CoreWeave 仍是市场衡量 AI 信贷⻛

险的核⼼标尺，是垃圾债市场 20 只项⽬融资数据中⼼债券中 7 只的租⼾，⽽试图跟随其上市的新
兴云计算公司也接连受挫。
谁的数学是对的？

的假设：⾼盛的答案是 195 亿⾄ 229 亿美元（含客⼾预付款），不含预付款则为 367 亿⾄ 845 亿美

告，或许正是这份反馈。
值得注意的是，⾼盛同时披露，其正担任 CoreWeave ⼀项待定承销业务的管理⼈或联席管理
⼈，持有该公司逾 1% 股份，并预计在未来三个⽉内寻求或获得投资银⾏服务报酬。正如伯恩斯
坦所⾔，账单终究要付，⽽第⼀笔 DDTL 5.0 的还款，就在此刻到期。
请发表您的评论
表情 发布评论

## Document 275: CTCP Nhựa Bình Minh - Báo cáo cập nhật 2Q2026 (2026-09-23)

Source PDF: all report pdf/CTCP Nhựa Bình Minh - Báo cáo cập nhật 2Q2026 (2026-09-23).pdf
Report source: KBSV (Company)
Date: 2026-09-23
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Doanh thu 2Q2026 tăng trưởng chậm 
nhưng biên lợi nhuận gộp vẫn được 
duy trì do tăng giá bán

Sản lượng tiêu thụ 6M2026 chững lại 
do tâm lý nhập hàng thận trọng và 
hoạt động xây dựng phục hồi chậm

Biên lợi nhuận gộp được dự báo sẽ 
thu hẹp cuối năm, phản ánh chi phí 
nguyên liệu cao hơn trong 1Q2026

Khuyến nghị TRUNG LẬP, giá mục 
tiêu 166,100 VND/cổ phiếu 
 Doanh thu/LNST 2Q2026 đạt lần lượt 1,320/367 tỷ VND (+1%/+11% YoY). Lũy kế 
6M2026, doanh thu đạt 2,777 tỷ VND (+3% YoY) trong khi LNST ước đạt 671 tỷ VND 
(+9% YoY). Biên lãi gộp 6M2026 cải thiện đạt 47.0% (+240 bps YoY), chủ yếu do việc 
tăng giá bán giúp doanh nghiệp bù đắp được mức tăng của chi phí đầu vào.

Lũy kế 6M2026, sản lượng tiêu thụ của BMP ước đạt 43,920 tấn, giảm 5% svck. Việc 
BMP thu hẹp các chương trình chiết khấu, với mức chiết khấu bình quân khoảng 
5.2% trong 2Q2026, làm giảm động lực nhập hàng của các đại lý và khiến sản lượng 
tiêu thụ chậm lại. Dù vậy, thị phần toàn quốc của BMP ước đạt 28% (+1 đpt svck) 
nhờ các doanh nghiệp khác trong ngành gặp khó khăn khi giá hạt nhựa tăng mạnh.

Biên lợi nhuận gộp của BMP dự kiến thu hẹp trong 2H2026 trước áp lực chi phí 
nguyên liệu đầu vào tăng. Tuy nhiên, chúng tôi kỳ vọng mức độ ảnh hưởng sẽ thấp 
hơn so với các doanh nghiệp trong ngành do (1) các lô nguyên liệu giá cao từ 
1Q2026 đã được đưa vào sản xuất và tiêu thụ một phần trong 1H2026; (2) BMP chủ 
động nâng gấp đôi hàng tồn kho để ứng phó với biến động bất lợi của giá nguyên 
liệu; (3) hơn 90% nhu cầu nguyên liệu uPVC được cung ứng từ các đối tác trong 
nước như hệ sinh thái SCG tại Việt Nam, AGC Chemicals và LSP; và (4) công ty duy 
trì giá bán bình quân ở mức cao.

Dựa trên kết quả kinh doanh và định giá, chúng tôi khuyến nghị TRUNG LẬP với cổ 
phiếu BMP, giá mục tiêu là 166,100 VND/cổ phiếu (tương ứng với mức tăng 14.3% 
so với giá đóng cửa ngày 23/09/2026).

Tăng trưởng doanh thu và lợi nhuận thuần 5 năm gần nhất Hoạt động kinh doanh

Nhựa Bình Minh (BMP) là doanh nghiệp hàng đầu 
trong lĩnh vực sản xuất ống nhựa với thị phần 
dẫn đầu tại thị trường nội địa (27% vào cuối 
2023), thị trường chính tập trung tại khu vực 
miền Nam. BMP có 4 nhà máy với tổng công suất 
đạt 150,000 tấn/năm. 
Nguồn: BMP, KBSV

Điểm nhấn đầu tư  Chú thích 
Kỳ vọng đà phục hồi của xây dựng dân dụng và bất động sản miền Nam sẽ thúc đẩy sản lượng tiêu 
thụ tăng trưởng trong 2026–2027. 
 Chi tiết ở phần dưới của báo cáo 
Giá PVC được kỳ vọng sẽ hạ nhiệt và khả năng giữ giá bán đầu ra ở mức cao giúp BMP giữ biên lợi 
nhuận gộp ở nền cao.

### Page 4

4

Nhu cầu nhập hàng thận trọng và 
cắt giảm các đợt chiết khấu trong 
kỳ của BMP làm giảm sản lượng tiêu 
thụ trong 2Q2026

Sự hồi phục của nhu cầu xây dựng 
dân dụng và nguồn cung các dự án 
bất động sản tại miền Nam sẽ thúc 
đẩy tăng trưởng sản lượng 2H2026 
 Sản lượng tiêu thụ trong 2Q2026 ước đạt 20,100 tấn, giảm 11% svck, nâng tổng 
sản lượng tiêu thụ 6M2026 ước đạt 43,920 tấn, giảm 5% svck do các yếu tố bất 
lợi từ bên ngoài như (1) giá hạt nhựa tăng cao khiến thị trường dè chừng trong 
việc nhập hàng vì lo ngại nguồn cung bất ổn (2) BMP đã thu hẹp các chương 
trình chiết khấu so với 2025 ngay trước thời điểm xung đột bùng nổ, giúp tỷ lệ 
chi phí bán hàng/doanh thu về mức 11.3%, giảm 800 bps so với 1Q2026 ở mức 
19.3%, nhưng đồng thời cũng khiến sản lượng tiêu thụ của BMP suy giảm do 
chính sách bán hàng thắt chặt hơn với các đối thủ có thị phần thấp hơn.

Chúng tôi kỳ vọng sản lượng tiêu thụ của BMP dần hồi phục từ 3Q2026 khi nhu 
cầu nhập hàng của các đại lý cải thiện sau giai đoạn thận trọng trong nửa đầu 
năm. Về cuối năm, hoạt động xây dựng dự kiến tăng tốc khi điều kiện thi công 
thuận lợi hơn sau mùa mưa. Bên cạnh đó, việc triển khai các dự án nhà ở tại 
TP.HCM và các tỉnh lân cận được kỳ vọng hỗ trợ nhu cầu ống nhựa trong giai 
đoạn tới. Theo đó, chúng tôi dự phóng sản lượng tiêu thụ của BMP lần lượt đạt 
96,695/100,125/96,750 tấn trong 2026/2027/2028.

Biểu đồ 3. Tương quan giữa tỷ lệ chi phí bán hàng/doanh 
thu và lợi nhuận thuần/sản lượng tiêu thụ  
 Biểu đồ 4. Dự phóng nguồn cung bất động sản tại Hà Nội và Hồ 
Chí Minh

Nguồn: BMP, KBSV  Nguồn: CBRE, KBSV

Biểu đồ 5. Vòng quay hàng tồn kho của BMP so với các doanh nghiệp trong ngành 
   BMP duy trì vòng quay dẫn đầu trong 
ngành nhờ lợi thế nguồn nguyên liệu và thị 
phần đứng đầu tại miền Nam. Tuy nhiên, 
xu hướng suy giảm các quý gần đây về 
mức 5.3 lần, giảm 0.21 điểm QoQ do nhu 
cầu tiêu thụ toàn ngành suy giảm.

### Page 5

5

Lợi thế từ hệ sinh thái công ty mẹ 
SCG và đối tác Nhật Bản giúp BMP 
có nhiều dư địa để nâng cao hiệu 
quả hoạt động

Theo chia sẻ từ ban lãnh đạo, BMP có lợi thế vì nhập nguyên liệu từ công ty mẹ 
là hệ sinh thái SCG tại Việt Nam và đối tác Nhật Bản là AGC Chemicals Vietnam, 
giúp giảm được 5% thuế nhập khẩu và chi phí vận chuyển. Điều này giúp BMP 
hưởng lợi kép khi có thể tăng được giá bán theo thị trường nhưng nguyên vật 
liệu rẻ hơn so với các doanh nghiệp trong ngành. KBSV nhận thấy định hướng 
dài hạn của BMP là cân bằng lợi ích cổ đông và khách hàng, vì vậy việc giảm giá 
bán để giành thị phần không phải lựa chọn ưu tiên của doanh nghiệp mà là 
nâng cao tỷ suất lợi nhuận/sản phẩm thành phẩm.

KBSV dự báo biên lợi nhuận gộp thu hẹp trong 2H2026 khi chi phí nguyên liệu 
giá cao từ 1H2026 được phản ánh vào giá vốn, và hồi phục trong 2027–2028 
nhờ đặc tính giữ giá bán ít thay đổi của nhóm nhựa. Chúng tôi kỳ vọng giá PVC 
resin chịu áp lực giảm từ (1) nhu cầu xây dựng tại Trung Quốc còn yếu khi thị 
trường bất động sản chưa hồi phục rõ ràng; và (2) các nhà sản xuất giải phóng 
hàng tồn kho sau mùa mưa, làm tăng sản lượng hạt nhựa PVC resin. Theo đó, 
KBSV dự phóng biên lợi nhuận gộp của BMP lên 46.4%/46.7%/46.5% trong 
2026/2027/2028.

gộp của BMP

## Document 278: De-reservification, Not De-dollarization (2026-09-15)

Source PDF: all report pdf/De-reservification, Not De-dollarization (2026-09-15).pdf
Report source: CFR (Follow the Money)
Date: 2026-09-15
Pages: 17
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1
EXPERT TAKE FOLLOW THE MONEY
De-reservification, Not De-dollarization
The world isn’t moving away from the dollar. It is shifting its dollars from traditional
reserves to state banks, pension funds, and other quasi-sovereign investors.
(Bernardo Montoya/Courtesy Reuters)
PUBLISHED
September 15, 2026 8:37 a.m.
Articles Follow the Money Share
Log In
Regions Topics Research Experts Events The Future of American Strategy

### Page 2

2
Brad W. Setser CFR EXPERT
Whitney Shepardson Senior Fellow
This is a joint blog with Alex Etra, Senior Macro
Strategist and head of Flow of Funds at Vanda
Research.
The IMF’s data on the currency composition of
foreign exchange (FX) reserves is scrutinized carefully
for any signs that the world is shifting way from the
dollar.
Yet it doesn’t really matter that much if the dollar’s
share of reserves has shifted from 56.5 percent to 57
percent. The ink-to-impact ratio of the quarterly

reporting on the latest COFER data is all off.
Neither the stock of global reserves nor the stock of
dollar reserves has changed much in the last ten years.
The action is elsewhere.

### Page 3

3
China’s state banks (per the Bank of International
Settlements) have almost as many foreign assets as the
central bank (PBOC).
Japan’s Government Pension Investment Fund
(GPIF) has almost as many foreign assets ($986
billion) as the government has FX reserves ($1.1
trillion at the end of August). Japan’s FX reserves are
on the books of the Ministry of Finance and the
GPIF is overseen by the Ministry of Health, Welfare
and Labor—so these are almost all assets of the

Government of Japan or the broader public sector,
not its central bank.
Korea’s National Pension Service has more foreign
assets than the Bank of Korea has FX reserves.
A different dynamic is in play in Taiwan, as the
government doesn’t run a big retirement fund. But in

### Page 4

4
a world where the hedge ratio of the life insurers has
been (and still is) used as a policy tool, the shift in
Taiwan’s foreign asset accumulation away from the
Central Bank of China (CBC) and over to
institutions whose activities in the foreign currency
market it directly influences broadly fits into the same
theme. So too does the PBOC’s use of the
“macroprudential adjustment factor” effectively adjust
banks’ net foreign assets in much the same way that

CBC regulations adjust lifer hedge ratios.
In the last year, the lifers’ hedge ratios—and their
direct hedges with the central bank—have been one
of the key tools the CBC has used to manage the
Taiwan dollar (and guard the lifers’ solvency).
Asia’s big surplus economies are in a sense just
catching up with the world’s oil exporters. Norway
put its oil surplus into a sovereign wealth fund from
the start. Kuwait, Qatar, and Abu Dhabi (the most
oil-rich emirate) all also have large sovereign funds.

And the world’s biggest oil exporter has joined them:
The Public Investment Fund has almost as many
foreign assets as the Saudi Central Bank (SAMA).*
What’s more, the available evidence suggests that the
dollar share of these pools is in line with or higher
than the dollar share of global FX reserves (it depends
on the institution and jurisdiction).

### Page 5

5
In other words, by looking at the (easily available)
data on the world’s static holdings of formal FX
reserve assets, scholars and analysts miss most of the
growth in the world’s sovereign and quasi-sovereign
foreign assets.
Consider China.
No serious analyst now disputes that China’s state
banks—including the policy banks (the China
Development Bank, China Exim)—hold several
trillion in foreign assets.
China reports $3.3 trillion in gross foreign assets to

the BIS (the net position, counting foreign bank
claims on the entire Chinese economy not just the
banks, is $2.5 trillion). That maps to the BOP data,
which shows almost $4 trillion in gross outflows
through the banking system (technically, the sum of
gross outflows in “other” plus the $500 billion in
foreign currency bonds held by the state commercial
banks)

### Page 6

6
Since 2010 (and even more so after 2014) the net
outflow through the state banks has exceeded reserve
accumulation.
The broad contours of this story are confirmed by the
balance sheet data reported by state commercial
banks in their 2025 annual reports, which showed

### Page 7

7
that the top five banks held a combined $2.5 trillion
in foreign currency assets (mostly held abroad).
The Chinese haven’t disclosed the foreign assets of
the two policy banks (with at this stage the complicity
of the IMF, which has neither analyzed the role of
SAFE policy bank financing, nor highlighted the
glaring gap in China’s own reporting). But the work
of AidData points to nearly $1 trillion in foreign
assets, with a hefty dollar share.
The available data sources all suggest that the bulk of

banks—puts the dollar share of their offshore foreign
currency assets at around 70 percent. That is well
above the last disclosed dollar share of China’s formal
reserves (55 percent in 2019). The banks’ annual

### Page 8

8
reports suggest that about 70 percent of the banks’
assets are in dollars and Hong Kong dollars (55
percent USD/15 percent HKD)—a bit below the
dollar and Hong Kong dollar share of their liabilities
(swaps out of the HKD fund a decent share of the
banks euro assets).
Put simply, SAFE’s static dollar holdings aren’t the
important story.
The real story is the rapid growth of the state banks
dollar holdings.
Japan holds a high (though undisclosed) dollar share

in its FX reserves, and those reserves are primarily
invested in Treasuries—so Japan’s MoF is now clearly
the largest contributor to the U.S. data on foreign
official holdings of Treasuries. (SAFE has shifted its
funds out of U.S. custodians, and thus increasingly
appears in the data as a “private” holder in a European
custodial center)
Japan’s formal reserves haven’t changed much in the
last ten years; periodic sales to limit the yen’s weakness
have offset most of the MoF’s accumulated interest

income. Foreign currency reserves were $1.2 trillion
in 2014 and $1.16 trillion at the end of Q1. They
dipped to about $1 trillion at the end of August after
sales of close to $100 billion at the end of July/start of
August.

### Page 9

9
What has grown is the foreign portfolio of the GPIF.
it was around $400 billion in 2014, ~$1 trillion in Q1
of 2026.
GPIF’s foreign bonds aren’t held exclusively in dollars,
but roughly 52 percent of its foreign bonds (26
percent of all bonds) are USD-denominated.
But the GPIF, like most international investors, holds
a large share of its equity portfolio in the U.S. (~$165
billion, 65 percent of the foreign equity portfolio).
Korea is a more extreme version of Japan.

The Bank of Korea’s FX reserves have been roughly
$375-450 billion over the last ten years or so. Around
two-thirds of those are now in dollars (as of the end of
2025).

### Page 10

10
From 2012 to 2025, the foreign assets at the NPS
have grown from ~$80 billion (around 21 percent of
total assets) to over $660 billion (55 percent of total
assets)—and something like 70 percent of those are in
dollars. 76 percent of the fund’s equities are in dollars,
and 66 percent of its debt. And while a lower share of
its “alternative” portfolio is in North America, most
alternative mandates are awarded in dollars.

### Page 11

11
In other words, the majority of the dollar assets held
by Korea’s government (the NPS reports to the
Ministry of Health and Welfare, and the Minister of
Health chairs its asset allocation committee) aren’t
held by the central bank and aren’t in the IMF’s
COFER data set.
Taiwan is an unusual case, as the life insurers aren’t
technically sovereign investors. But with their
regulator now explicitly encouraging the them to hold
an unhedged foreign currency book and hence they

now rely on the state for their solvency, they have a bit
of an official character (a significant appreciation of
the TWD would blow through their volatility reserves
and leave them with fewer assets than liabilities, as
their foreign assets are largely held against TWD
policies). The CBC certainly now uses the lifers desire
to unwind existing hedges to help manage the Taiwan

### Page 12

12
dollar. And they have an exceptionally high dollar
share in their foreign assets—over 95 percent.
The Saudi Public Investment Fund (PIF) is another
example.
It keeps roughly $200 billion of its $250 billion in
foreign investments in dollars—well above the U.S.
share of a standard global stock index. Almost all of
the PIF’s $30 billion in external debt is also in dollars.
And the PIF now has only slightly fewer foreign
assets than the Saudi Central Bank has reserves.**

The broad story is thus pretty clear: the growth in the
world’s sovereign and quasi-sovereign assets is not
coming through an increase in FX reserve holdings
managed by the world’s central banks.

### Page 13

13
And, in the critical case of China, the dollar share of
China’s quasi-sovereign foreign assets is likely higher
than the dollar share of their formal FX reserves.
Adam Tooze has noted that inflows into the U.S. are
now coming from investors looking for a profit, not
investors looking for safety. What he calls the “profit
dollar” manifests itself in a number of different ways.
More inflows into corporate bonds and equities for
one. Less official demand for Treasuries for another.

More opacity too.
Those same trends are at play among sovereign and
quasi-sovereign investors.
So don’t obsess about the dollar’s chare in formal FX
reserves. Do recognize that the dollar’s “reserve
currency role” isn’t the source of any significant new
inflows into the dollar.

### Page 14

14
The dollar’s dominant role in the international
monetary system depends on much more than the
size and composition of central bank FX reserves. It is
as much, perhaps more so (given that FX reserve
managers are themselves ultimately liability matchers)
a function of the portfolio choice of a set of
private/semi-private/quasi-sovereign investors that
are much more difficult to observe. Reserve currency
status is not only about FX reserves in a strict sense.

That’s been true for some time. Most of the current
flow into U.S. from the “official” sector is coming
from investors who are not classic reserve managers.
Those flows remain heavily tilted toward the dollar, at
least for now.***
And any real de-dollarization would likely occur first
among these investors.
Put differently, the dollar’s global role is increasingly
as a source of returns, not a source of safety. The
foreign bid, private and public, is for risk, not for

Treasuries. That doesn’t help Scott Bessent much
right now, but it has helped keep valuations in the
stock market extended. And the global debate on the
dollar’s role lags the evolution in the dollar’s role, and
the risks associated with that new role.
It is currently fashionable in some circles to point to
the diminution of the “convenience yield” on

### Page 15

15
Treasuries even while the U.S. dollar continues to
enjoy such a privilege. But the shift in the global
investor base, and their apparent portfolio preference
for U.S. risk assets in lieu of Treasuries, may help
explain the divergence between the convenience yield
on UST and the convenience yield on USD.
And of course, if the negative convenience yield
implies that Treasuries are trading at a historical
discount, the persistence of the latter—and its

concentration in risk assets—suggests that the “profit
dollar” itself is likely trading at a historical premium.
That apparent premium on U.S. risk assets deserves as
much (or more) scrutiny as the currency composition
of central bank FX reserves.
* The PIF and Saudi Aramco also now have a certain
amount of debt.
** Reserves (now mostly in Treasuries, so should
appear in the TIC data, unlike at some times in the
past.

### Page 16

16
This work represents the views solely of the author(s). The
Council on Foreign Relations is an independent,
nonpartisan membership organization, think tank, and

## Document 282: ECB caught in the French drama

Source PDF: all report pdf/ECB caught in the French drama.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-22
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

The European Central Bank meeting on 29 October is not
expected to result in an interest rate hike. Instead, the French
crisis is increasingly eroding the need for additional rate hikes
Carsten Brzeski
With the surge in bond yields on the back of the crisis in France, we expect the
ECB to remain on hold at next week's meeting
Back in September, the ECB surprised markets with a more hawkish
message, at least between the lines. With headline inﬂation higher

for longer and core inﬂation expected to increase in 2027, the stage
seemed set for at least one more rate hike at the December
meeting. Some even speculated about a back-to-back increase in
October.
One month later, and after a severe ﬁscal and political crisis in
France, the ECB is unlikely to repeat the hawkish tone at next week’s
meeting. At face value, macro developments since the last meeting
would still justify another rate hike. Headline inﬂation increased

again in September and most leading indicators point to continued
resilience in the eurozone economy. However, the surge in bond
Cookie settings
At ING we respect your right to choose how your data is used.

which you prefer to disable. Once you've made your selection,
please conﬁrm your preferences. You can change your cookie

Essential

Conﬁrm selection Accept all

### Page 2

yields on the back of the French crisis is already imposing a new
restrictive monetary policy stance. In fact, the surge in German
bond yields since the September meeting has already had roughly
the same impact on growth and inﬂation as an additional 25bp
rate hike. For France, it's closer to a 50bp hike. Or in other words, the
bond market has taken over the ECB’s job.
All of this means that the ECB is very likely to stay on hold next
week and will delay any next rate hike decision to December. This is

when new staﬀ projections will be available and when there will
also be more insights into whether higher energy prices will
eventually bring knock-on eﬀects on the rest of the economy or
not. In fact, there are three main parameters that, in our view, will
determine the ECB’s path going forward: i) the length and intensity
of the war in the Middle East. The longer oil prices remain elevated,
the higher the probability of more rate hikes; ii) the knock-on eﬀects

of higher energy prices. So far, there has hardly been any pass-
through of higher energy prices in the rest of the economy. If this
remains the case, the need for further rate hikes drops. If it doesn’t,
the likelihood of new rate hikes goes up. iii) France. Any solution to
the current French ﬁscal woes could bring some relief to bond
markets and allow the ECB to continue hiking rates. Without any
solution, bond markets have taken over the ECB’s role, making rate

hikes less likely (and less needed).
Because it's France
Even if France does not play a role in the ECB’s policy statement
next week, it will be an important element at the press conference.
Political and ﬁscal concerns in France, and the resulting widening in
French spreads, have raised questions about whether the ECB could
intervene through the Transmission Protection Instrument (TPI),
which allows it to purchase securities issued by member states

whose ﬁnancing conditions have deteriorated for reasons not
justiﬁed by country-speciﬁc fundamentals. However, before
activating it, the Governing Council considers a cumulative set of
criteria to determine whether the countries concerned are pursuing
sound and sustainable ﬁscal and macroeconomic policies. In
practice, this includes compliance with the EU ﬁscal framework,

### Page 3

which would, at a minimum, require the proposed French budget to
be fully adopted.
The ECB has nevertheless left itself some discretion, stating that the
intervention criteria “will be dynamically adjusted to the unfolding
risks and conditions to be addressed”. This brings back memories of
the famous “because it’s France” by former European Commission
President Jean-Claude Juncker. Generally speaking, the ECB will be
in a diﬃcult position: intervening too quickly in support of France

may be viewed unfavourably by other member states and
interpreted as monetary ﬁnancing of the debt of a country
unwilling to follow the rules. That argues strongly against early
intervention. Also, don’t forget that the leadership changes at the
ECB over the next months (Schnabel, Lane, Lagarde) could also
aﬀect how the ECB views TPI.
Given the challenges of justifying the activation of TPI at present, we
expect the ECB to adopt a three-pronged approach to address the

French crisis.
1. The ﬁrst line of defence is a more dovish approach to policy
rates. As bond markets are currently taking over the ECB’s job
of inserting a more restrictive monetary stance on the
economy, there is less need for the ECB to hike rates
aggressively, or to hike rates at all. In fact, the ECB has already
started to prepare for this option, as ECB president Christine
Lagarde recently noted that “while growth has been resilient,
since our last meeting long-term interest rates have risen

notably, which will slow growth and reduce pass-through by
more than projected in our September exercise".
2. The second line of defence would be a covert return to bond
purchases. The ECB could instead pause quantitative tightening
temporarily and reinvest maturing bonds in its portfolio
'ﬂexibly', sending a positive signal to bond markets. This had
already happened during the Covid period.
3. Last but not least, should contagion in eurozone bond markets

intensify, we expect the ECB to eventually trigger the TPI. As
long as France remains non-compliant with the European ﬁscal

rules, the ECB might even consider purchasing bonds from
almost all eurozone countries, except for France.

All in all, next week’s ECB meeting looks set to be uneventful on

## Document 305: French business confidence defies the turmoil for now

Source PDF: all report pdf/French business confidence defies the turmoil for now.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

October’s unexpected rebound in business conﬁdence is good
news, particularly for industry. But the survey does not yet
capture the impact of the latest social unrest, while higher
interest rates, a weak labour market and deteriorating public
ﬁnances are clouding the outlook further
Charlotte de Montpellier
An unexpected improvement
Against all expectations, business conﬁdence edged up in October
despite the sharp rise in French interest rates since September,

gaining one point to reach 97. It therefore remains below its long-
term average, but the rebound is a welcome surprise in the current
environment.
The main surprise came from industry, where the indicator jumped
four points to 105. The increase was largely concentrated in
transport equipment and mainly reﬂected an improvement in order
books, particularly foreign orders. The other components also
improved, but much more modestly. In services, the rebound was

### Page 2

limited to one point, taking the indicator to 99. This mainly
reﬂected stronger expectations for activity and demand, as well as
a marked recovery in administrative and support services. By
contrast, conﬁdence deteriorated in retail and construction.
On prices, the survey provides no new inﬂationary signal. Intentions
to raise prices declined in industry and remained stable in both
services and construction.
The rebound is encouraging, especially after several months of

disappointing industrial data. But it does not fundamentally alter
the outlook. The indicator of perceived economic uncertainty rose
sharply. In services, it even reached its highest level outside the
Covid-19 crisis.
More importantly, the employment climate fell three points to 95,
driven by a clear deterioration in hiring intentions in services
excluding temporary employment. The signal from the labour
market therefore remains ﬁrmly negative, which should limit

growth in household income and consumption over the coming
quarters.
Finally, the survey was conducted between late September and
early October. It therefore captures the widening in French spreads,
but not the violence and social unrest seen last weekend. October’s
rebound is encouraging, but fragile and already partly overtaken by
events.
Social unrest is already changing the picture
Since the October business conﬁdence data was collected, the
nature of the risk has changed. It no longer relates only to weak

activity and the impact of higher interest rates, but also to the
economic, political and ﬁnancial consequences of social unrest.
The economic cost of the violence is not limited to broken shop
windows and looting. As explained here, material damage
represents only the most visible part of the bill. Closures, lower
footfall and permanently lost sales could weigh far more heavily on
activity.

### Page 3

But the real issue is persistence. A brief and localised episode would
probably have a limited macroeconomic impact, while prolonged
unrest could undermine conﬁdence by encouraging households to
avoid city centres, tourists to postpone their trips and businesses to
delay investment. This could turn a temporary disruption into a
more lasting economic shock.
The unrest also makes it even more diﬃcult to secure
parliamentary approval for the budget, at a time when the

government has virtually no room to respond through additional
spending or tax cuts. France therefore has neither the ﬁscal room to
buy social peace nor the political room to impose the necessary
adjustment easily.
The vicious circle is under way
The reaction in ﬁnancial markets shows that the conﬁdence
channel is already at work. The yield on 10y French OATs has
crossed the symbolic 5% threshold, while the spread over the
German Bund has widened beyond 160bp, from 137bp last Friday.

These moves reﬂect both growing doubts about the ability of any
future government to pass the necessary reforms and savings
measures, and mounting concerns about the outlook for the French
economy.
This creates a vicious circle. At the same time, the renewed
deterioration in ﬁnancial conditions is further weakening an already
very poor growth outlook by increasing the cost of credit. We expect
GDP growth of just 0.3% in 2026 and 0.6% in 2027, but the risks are

tilted towards another downward revision. Against this backdrop,
the government’s objective of reducing the deﬁcit to 5% of GDP in
2027 looks particularly fragile, as it is based on growth of 0.5% in
2026 and 1% in 2027, well above our forecast. Even if the draft
budget were adopted in full, it might therefore not be enough, as
weaker activity would automatically reduce the expected tax
revenues.
A rating downgrade would merely conﬁrm
the obvious
